Problem: Find a step function h: [0,2] → [0,1] that minimizes C_5 = max_k ∫ h(x)(1-h(x+k)) dx, with ∫h = 1.

Note: the evaluation uses np.correlate(h, 1-h, mode="full") * dx, and takes the max. Note that np.correlate(a, v) = sum(a[m+i]*conj(v[i])), so the shift direction may be off, but since we take the max over all shifts, direction doesn't matter.

The current record is C5 ≤ 0.38085, and the previous program was 0.380956, using n=256.

Hmm, by change of variables using periodicity: ∫_0^2 h(x - k) dx = 1 (under periodic boundary conditions, if we treat [0,2] as a circle, the integral over the full period doesn't depend on the shift). Then ∫_0^2 h(x)(1-h(x+k)) dx = ∫ h(x) dx - ∫ h(x)h(x+k) dx = 1 - S(k), where S(k) = ∫ h(x)h(x+k) dx is the autocorrelation.

So g(k) = 1 - S(k). And the average of S(k) over k: (1/2)∫_0^2 S(k) dk = ... ∫_0^L f(x+k) dk = ∫_0^L f(u) du =: M (independent of x, on the torus). So the double integral = ∫ f(x) M dx = M^2. Therefore average of S(k) = M^2 / L = 1/2 (when L=2, M=1).

Wait, let me check. Actually the objective max is over k of ∫ h(x)(1-h(x+k)) dx, but x is integrated over [0,2]…hmm, with what domain? In discretization: h has n samples on [0,2], dx=2/n. np.correlate(h, 1-h, 'full') gives contributions at all shifts, but due to boundary effects, for large shifts the overlap region shrinks. The integral ∫ h(x)(1-h(x+k)) dx is over the domain where both h(x) and h(x+k) are defined. On a half-line, if k is between [0,2], the domain is x ∈ [0, 2-k], and if k is [-2, 0], it's [−k, 2].

So the actual definition: ∫ h(x)(1-h(x+k)) dx, over x where both are defined. For 0 ≤ k ≤ 2 that's ∫_0^{2-k}. For negative k similarly.

So my torus computation doesn't directly apply. Also, the reported C5=0.38 is below 1/2, so indeed the integral is only over the intersection: for a large shift k, the domain is small and the integral is small. So the max occurs at intermediate shifts.

Also k=2 (full shift): zero domain, so integral is 0.

So C5 = max over k ∈ [-2, 2] of ∫_{x: x, x+k ∈ [0,2]} h(x)(1-h(x+k)) dx.

To minimize the max of g(k) = 1 - ...hmm no: ∫_{0}^{2-k} h(x) dx - ∫_0^{2-k} h(x)h(x+k) dx. The first term is the mass of h in [0, 2-k], which is between 0 and 1.

Interesting: to minimize the max, we want h(x+k) = 1 wherever h(x) = 1, for the worst k. That's a correlation structure.

Swap-based local search: swap a 1 at i with a 0 at j. The change in each g_k at each shift can be computed from a sparse set of affected shifts: the change to g_{j-i} etc. Actually the change in overlap happens at shifts k where the pair's relationship changes: the shifts k in {i-j, j-i, i (and all i - y for y...hmm. Let me think: g_k depends on the pairs (x, x+k). If we toggle h at positions i (1→0) and j (0→1), pairs affected: for each x, x=k)…a pair (x, x+k) is affected if x ∈ {i,j} or x+k ∈ {i,j}. So the affected shifts: k = i - x and x = i - k…for each affected pair, k ranges over: for x=i: k = all x such that (i, i+k) is a pair → k = y - i (y is any position) → all k? No wait, pairs involving position i as the first element: (i, i+k) for all k such that i+k is in range → k ∈ [-(i), n-1-i]...that's O(n) shifts, each with one pair. Similarly for position j as first: O(n). And as second element: (i-k, i) with x = i-k in range: k ∈ [i-n+1, i] → O(n). So a swap affects O(n) shifts, each with one pair, giving a total O(n) updates to the g_k array. With g_k maintained incrementally, a full evaluation is O(n). For n=256 that's very fast: ~512 float operations per trial → ~1-2 million trials per second in Python, maybe 500k/s. Now let me design the actual algorithm carefully to fit 1300 sec, 1 CPU, pure numpy + scipy (scipy/cvxpy allowed, but something like CBC is probably overkill since it's a discrete max problem).

Main performance goal: evaluate and propose swaps at a high rate.

Incremental objective maintenance for binary h (length n, exactly n/2 ones):

Let each index 0..n-1 represent x = i*dx. g_k = Σ_{i: 0≤i<n, 0≤i+k<n} h_i (1 - h_{i+k}), for k ∈ -(n-1)..(n-1). Stored in an array C of length 2n-1, C[k+n-1] = g_k (count of integer). c5 = C.max() * (2/n).

Suggested move: choose 1 at i, 0 at j, toggle both. Delta as derived:
- For each z ∈ Z, z ≠ i: C[z - i] -= 1 (pair (i, i+k=z-i) no longer contributes) — wait, these pairs were contributing only if h_z = 0 (z ∈ Z ✓) and h_i = 1 ✓ → yes all pairs (i, z)?? But the shift is k = z - i, which can be negative or positive — but the pair (x=i, x+k=z): in our g_k formula k = z - i, whatever. But careful: when z < i, "pair (i, i+k)": i+k = z < i: that's g_{z-i} = Σ_{i'} h_{i'}(1-h_{i'+z-i}), contributing i'=i term: h_i(1-h_z) = 1 (z ∈ Z). ✓. So all z ∈ Z (z≠i): C[z - i]--. That's |Z| - 1 updates to "different" shifts (each z gives a different shift).
- For each u ∈ U, u ≠ i: C[i - u] += 1 (pair (u, i): h_u = 1, h_i before = 1 → didn't contribute; after h_i = 0 → contributes). Shift k = i - u.
- For each z ∈ Z, z ≠ j: C[z - j] += 1 (pair (j, z): now h_j = 1, h_z = 0 → contributes; before h_j=0 → didn't).
- For each u ∈ U, u ≠ j: C[j - u] -= 1 (pair (u, j): before h_u = 1, h_j = 0 → contributed; after h_j = 1 → no).
- Cross fix: pair (i, j): handled in the rule 1 (z=j: C[j-i]-- ...wait z=j∈Z, z≠i → C[j-i] -= 1) and in rule 4 (u=i ∈ U, u ≠ j → C[j - i] -= 1) → total -2; true: pair (i,j) pre: h_i=1, h_j=0 contributes → 1; post h_i=0 → 0. Change -1 → so add +1 at shift j-i.
- Pair (j, i): rule 2 (u=j: C[i - j] += 1) and rule 3 (z=i: C[i - j] += 1) → +2; true: pre h_j=0 → no; post h_j=1, h_i=0 → yes → +1 → subtract 1 at i - j... Correction: after applying the -2 and +2, add +1 at j-i, -1 at i-j.

Alternatively, more simply: apply 4 rules, then C[j-i] += 1, C[i-j] -= 1. No, sign: at j-i: rules gave -1 (rule 1: z=j) and -1 (rule 4: u=i) = -2; truth -1; correction +1 ✓. At i-j: rule 2 (u=j → C[i-j]+=1) and rule 3 (z=i → C[i-j]+=1) = +2; truth +1; correction -1 ✓.

Total updates per trial: (|Z|-1) + (|U|-1) + (|Z|-1) + (|U|-1) + 2 = 2n - 4 + 2 = 2n - 2. At n=1024: 2046 element updates, each to a different shift. Implementation: build 4 numpy arrays: shifts1 = Z_arr - i (excluding z=i), etc., each ~n/2; use np.add.at(C, shifts + offset, ±1). Each np.add.at ~ (n/2) additions — np.add.at is somewhat slow (~50 ns/op? probably more, ~100-200ns). 2046 * 150ns ≈ 310µs per trial → 3k trials/sec. Too slow? 1300 sec → 4 million trials — barely acceptable, but the np.add.at overhead (4 calls + array-creation each ~n/2) is high: building (n-1) arrays of shifts per trial ~ 2 * 512 float per trial: 4 µs. Let me proceed with a numerical design in a principled way:

### Algorithm design (final)

- Work with binary patterns U ⊂ {0..n-1}, |U| = n/2. Use n ∈ {768, 1024, 1536?} mainly; or maybe n = 512 first. Actually let's do 1024 main + 512 sanity.

- Incremental C array with O(2n) updates per trial as derived, using a small precomputed mapping: arrays U_arr, Z_arr (int32). Per trial (i ∈ U, j ∈ Z):
  shift offsets:
  base = n - 1
  idx1 = Z_arr - i + base (mask z != i) → decrement all except z=i → simplest: decrement whole Z (|Z| = n/2) then single increment at pos i? I.e., do np.add.at(C, Z + base - i, -1), then C[base + (i - i)]...hmm the excluded pairs: z = i in rule 1 (self) — z never equals i since i ∈ U. Wait, set: Z is i's complement, so z ≠ i is automatic! I was being overly paranoid: z ∈ Z excludes i (i ∈ U). U excludes j. So the 4 rules are:
  - C[Z - i + base] -= 1 (count n/2)
  - C[i - U + base] += 1 (n/2)
  - C[Z - j + base] += 1 (n/2)
  - C[j - U + base] -= 1 (n/2)
  - Cross fix: +1 at j - i + base, -1 at i - j + base...
  Wait, verify cross fix with self-exclusion: pair (i, j): rule 1: z = j ∈ Z ✓ → C[j - i] -= 1 ✓. Rule 2: u = j ∈ U ✓ → C[i - j] += 1?? wait rule 2 is hitting z for u ∈ U: at shift i - u, u=j: C[i - j] += 1. Rule 3: z = i ∈ Z ✓: C[i - j] += 1. Rule 4: u = i ∈ U: C[j - i] -= 1.
  So at shift j - i: rule 1 (-1) + rule 4 (-1) = -2; truth: pair (i,j) contribution 1 → 0: -1 → fix +1 at j - i ✓.
  Shift i - j: rule 2 (+1) + rule 3 (+1) = +2; truth: 0 → 1: +1 → fix -1 at i - j ✓.
  All consistent.

  Vectorize: precompute Z_i = Z_arr — but shift is trial-dependent (Z - i): building 4 arrays of n/2 elements per trial = 2n int ops → trivial for numpy (~10µs each? or (Z - i) array is ~5µs). Use np.add.at on C. 4 add.at calls ≈ 4 × (n/2 atoms ~150ns?) ≈ 300µs / try at n=1024. Hmm.

  Alternative faster: **precompute a per-position delta structure**? Update = addat(C, Z - i, -1) + addat(C, U - i, +1) + addat(C, Z - j, +1) + addat(C, j - U, -1) + fix. Notice: adds with i: -[C at Z-i] + [C at U-i]: combined = addat(C, U - i, +1) + addat(C, Z - i, -1) = a full vector y - i (add +1 to all shifts y - i for y ∈ U? all y? no, only U vs Z ±1 — that's "over all y" 0 × ?; decompose as +1 for all y, then -1 extra for Z's and +1 for U's extra: = (sum on all?)no: Σ_U δ_{y-U} - Σ_Z δ_{y-Z} = (Σ_all δ)(0): Σ_U - Σ_Z = f(y) with each value is ± 1 depending on position: the pattern is a full circle of ± 1 values: for all y: f(y - i), f = +1 on U, -1 on Z. So the i-part = convolve: addat(C, y - i, f(y)) — with top sign f: base = -1 everywhere; +1 for y ∈ U. So i-part = (addat(C, all - i, -1)) + (addat(C, U - i, +1)). Full-span (length 2n-1, kernel shift range -(n-1)..(n-1) — y - i ranges over i - (n-1)..(n-1 - i), within array bounds ✓ scanning all y) always valid, constant vector → C[t] += -1 where t ∈ [base - (i - 0)??]: y - i from y=0..n-1 → offsets: i - (n-1) to i: so an interval of length n: C[base + i - (n-1) : base + i + 1] -= 1 (a contiguous slice!). Nice: the full-span "-1 on all y" part is a "contiguous" slice decrement of length n! Similarly +1 on U part: only |U| = n/2 adds at positions U - i (non-contiguous).
  Total per trial:
  - Slice op: 2 (for i's full -1, j's full ...wait j-part: with j ↔ +1 on U: +1 slice on range (j - (n-1))..j and +addat(C, Z - j, -1)?? Symmetric: j-part = +1 on z ∈ Z?? Earlier rules: C[Z - j] += 1 (rule 3) and C[j - U] -= 1 (rule 4) = +(kernel +1 on Z, -1 on U) at shift y - j, sign g(y), g = +1 on Z, -1 on U. So "g(y) = -f(y)" (where f = +1 U, -1 Z): j-part = -(full slice +1) + addat(U - j, +1)?? i.e., -( here sign g): g(y) = -Σ...decompose g = -1 on U + 1 on Z = -(+1 on U) + (all +1): j-part = addat(slice [base + j - (n-1) .. base + j], +1) + addat(C, U - j, -1).   Then cross fix: +1 at j - i, -1 at i - j.

Budget allocation (total 1300s; leave 20-30s for final refinement/serialization):
- Phase A (~100-200s): multi-start binary search at n = 512 and n = 1024: starts: (1) parent upsample-threshold, (2) Thue-Morse, (3) "anti-symmetric 1 - h" search, (4) a few random. Each start: climb (greedy + plateau L-helped) until stall, then kick-restart within a neighborhood of the best-so-far. Track global best.
- Phase B (~300-500s): heavy SA/hill climb at n=1024 and n=1536? from the best of A, with biased worst-shift proposals + multi-shuffle restarts. Also "structural kick" moving 5-15 pairs.
- Phase C (~150-250s): continuous refinement: from top binaries (n=1024), scipy L-BFGS-B smooth-max (β anneal 20→100), constraint Σh = n/2, box. Evaluate final strict c5 per refine.
- Phase D (remainder): repetition/improvement of the best result: small i.e.: repair and final continuous micro-refine of C-max at the top score (n ≤ ~500).

The full continuous refinement isn't a dead end but risky for time; at least include a cheap "1-opt fractional polish": for each i, binary h_i, move δ into h_i, and spread -δ uniformly over a free set F (cells in (0,1)): pick free set = currently near-binary interior cells...trivial scheme: for each i, max giving improvement: polynomial-ish; O(n^2) evaluations with fast incremental... 1-sec too conservative. Now, performance details of core search loop (critical path):

State: n; U: np.ndarray int32 (ones positions); Z: np.ndarray int32 (zeros); C: np.ndarray int32 length (2n - 1); m = C.max(); L = (C == m).sum().

Per trial:
 - Pick i (from U), j (from Z) (cheap: rng indices into arrays).
 - Build proposed at D: fastest path without full zero allocation: pre-allocate a scratch D (2n-1) int32, and zero out only touched regions via tracking...to fully zero a 2047-int array per trial (~1µs) is fine.
   os = Ufull array: UB = (U + base) int32, length n/2.
   D[:] = 0
   D[si0:si1] = -1, si0 = base + i - (n-1), si1 = base + i + 1 (length n)
   D[sj0:sj1] = +1
   np.add.at(D, UB - i, 1)
   np.add.at(D, UB - j, -1)
   D[base + j - i] += 1
   D[base + i - j] -= 1
   newC = C + D (pre-allocated NC)
   m2 = NC.max()
   if m2 < m or (m2 == m and L2 < L): accept: C[:], NC[])? Actually swap arrays: C, NC = NC, C; Z,U swap membership: find the index of i in U and remove, swap in...maintaining U as an array: swap i and positions: use position map: U has a U_pos array (index in U of position p), and Z_pos similarly. Swap: U[U_pos[i]] ↔ j:
     ui = U_pos[i]; uj = Z_pos[j]
     U[ui] = j; Z[uj] = i
     U_pos[i] = uj; U_pos[j] = ui
     Z_pos[j] = ui; Z_pos[i] = uj
     UB[ui] = j + base; UB[?]: UB = U + base: update UB[ui] = j + base ✓ (keep UB sync, or compute UB from U when needed: a U + base array op per critical patch is 512-float ~1µs — precompute and keep in sync).
     m = m2; L = L2 (L2 = (NC == m2).sum() count: full scan 2047 ~1-2µs; only on accept — plateaus frequent but...) ok.
   else reject (nothing to do, NC/D is scratch).

   When...for L2 if we need L2 on each trial (even on reject) with m2 == m for tiebreak: plateau trials we need C==m2 count anyway: cheap full scan; otherwise skip compute (assume L2 is big → reject): logic: if m2 < m: accept, L2 = count.   Total D ops per trial: fill (2047) 0.3µs + slice -1 (1024) 0.2 + slice +1 (1024) 0.2 + G slice add (2047) 1 + G slice sub (2047) 1 + 2 scalars 0.1 → ~3µs; C + D 0.5; max 0.4; slice-scan on accept maybe; Python overhead ~2-4µs → ~7-9µs / trial → ~120k sec^-1. If 100k/sec → 100M+ trials over 1300s. Easily beat the previous search budget, by 10-100×.

Even subtler: skip D.fuller; overlap G slice with C + G? Candidate = C + D: at t: C[t] - 1 (t ∈ Si) + 1 (t ∈ Sj) + G_i(t) - G_j(t) + fix. Compute M2 = max of that expression...D build is cleanest; fine.

Move proposals:
 - Default: i random U, j random Z. Uniform random pairs: 262k possible at n=1024 — that's the neighborhood; 100k trials/sec = 1 full neighborhood pass per 2.6s. Greedy should converge to a strong local optimum in a few minutes.
 - Biased mode: pre-compute worst-shift list argmax (track a m count positions). Biased proposal: pick a k* (from the top L), find all pairs (x, x + k*) with h_x = 1, h_{x+k*} = 0 (blame pairs of C[k*]): the set B_{k*} = {x ∈ U: h_{x + k*} = 0 (valid)}. Sample x from B (i.e., i), j = x + k* (force j)? That's a specific swap that kills a blame pair: i → j with j = i + k* (effect: -1 for C[k*], +1 for C[−k*]...signs: C[k*]: pair (i, j) destroyed −1; pair (i − k*, i) created? after j...: h_i = 0: pair (i − k*, i) (1,0) created +1 (if h_{i-k*} = 1, though reversed? sign: contribution (i − k*, i), h(i−k*) = 1, h(i) = 0 counts in C[k*] ✓ +1 (if h_{i-k*} = 1); pre-sets pair (j, j + k*): h_j = 0 → no; post h_j = 1: pair (j, j+k*): if h_{j+k*} = 0, contributes +1; and (j − k*, j) = (i, j): destroyed. Net C[k*]: 1 + [h_{i-k*} = 1] + [h_{j+k*} = 0] − 1 = [h_{i-k*} = 1] + [h_{j+k*} = 0] ∈ {0, 1, 2}. So even if neighbors bad the pair cannot reduce C[k*]; a random j is better. Biased proposal just i ∈ B_{k*} random, j random Z — would, h_{i+k*} = 0? Choosing j randomly might give a +1 in the bad neighborhood offsetting...net: C[k*] change = −b_i + c_j, b_i = 1 + [h_{i-k*} = 1] (wait b_i is true contribution of i in k*: pair (i, i+k*) ∈ U, Z: b = 1 + [h_{i-k*} = 1]?? So b_i = 1 + [h_{i-k*} = 1] ∈ {1, 2}. c_j (j added, from all view? for C[k*]): pair (j, j + k*) created: +[h_{j+k*} = 0]; pair (j − k*, j) created: +[h_{j-k*} = 1]...hmm pre h_j = 0: no: post: (j − k*, j): h_{j-k*} = 1 and h_j = 1? no wait (x, x + k*) = (j - k*, j): loser when h_{j-k*} = 1 (h(j) = 1 post) → no contribution (need 1, 0): h_j = 1 → contributes 0. So c_j = [h_{j + k*} = 0 (valid)] ∈ {0, 1}. ΔC[k*] -= (b_i − c_j) ∈ {−1, 0, +1}: with i ∈ B we need j such that c_j = 0, i.e., h_{j+k*} = 1 (based on the pair of j): sample j from Z subject to h_{j+k*} = 1 — those are "good zeros for k*": pre-images? For C[k*] −1, need h_{j+k*} = 1 but j ∈ Z: adjacent to the right with a 1: likely to exist if there are pairs. Then ΔC[k*] = −b_i, ΔC[−k*]: mirror: from i U removal: −[h_{i-k*} = 1]...both sides symmetric: ΔC[−k*] = −([h_{i-k*} = 1] ...wait C[−k*] = Σ_x h_x(1 − h_{x-k*}): i ∈ U: (i, i - k*): loser if h_{i-k*} = 0: i removal: −[h_{i-k*} = 0]: and (i + k*, i): created +[h_{i+k*} = 1]. So ΔC[−k*] = −[h_{i-k*} = 0] + [h_{j-k*} = 0] − [h_{j+k*} = 1](−1) = if h_{i-k*} = 0 (b_i = 1) and h_{j-k*} = 0, h_{j+k*} = 1: 1 + 0 − 1: 0 → good: single kill. To C[k*] −1, need h_{j+k*} = 1, h_{j-k*} = 0, h_{i-k*} ∈ {1} (then ΔC−k* = 0 + [0] − 1 = −1? So "clean kill" proposal: sample k* from active; find i ∈ U with h_{i+k*} = 0, h_{i-k*} = 1?? And j = ...otherwise not structurally fixed: j ∈ Z with h_{j+k*} = 1, h_{j-k*} = 0, j ∉ {i+k*} (i + k* ∈ Z ✓ (i ∈ B), and h_{i+k* + k*}? this is the last condition...sample a pool of j: Z_k* = {j ∈ Z: h_{j+k*} = 1 (valid), h_{j-k*} = 0 (valid)}. If pool empty, random j. This targeted sampler aims to strictly reduce the count of max shifts → strong descent direction when active set is small.

 - "Anti" flip with h' = 1 - h: consider complement transformation: h' = 1 − h (same integral): g_{h', k} = Σ(1 − h_x)(h_{x+k}) = M'(k) - D'(k), M' = (2 − |k|)·...hmm M'(k) = mass'(domain) = (length - mass): g_{h'} (k) = N(k) − D(k), N(k) = #domain pairs; D' (k) = Σ h_{x+k} - Σ h_x h_{x+k}...= N(k) − D_{h}(k)?? recompute: g_{h'} (k) = Σ_{x ∈ I_k} (1 − h_x) h_{x+k} = Σ h_{x+k} − Σ h_x h_{x+k} = M(k of shifted domain) − D(k). M_shifted = mass of I_k + k = (2 − |k|) − mass(I_k) (counts: (n - |k|) dx − M dx): in counts: c5'(k) = ((n - |k|) − M(k)) − D(k) times dx. So h' is roughly complementary to h in the sense of swapping target shifts. If h is near-upper, then h' is also an upper competitor: use both. Note the sum constraint of h' is automatically satisfied ✓. So generate symmetric starts. (C array is cheap to recompute on full recompute via formula.)

 - Structural "kick": after stall: pick r = 5..20 random pairs and apply blindly (updating state), then re-climb. Or "batch apply of all improving moves": standard "Apply-all-improving in random order" (deterministic-like "1-pass descent"), a single pass over all good-U-random order...trials per pass = 512 × something — implementation: iterate i over shuffled U (512); for each i, best j in a small random sample (say 8) by m2? That's 512 × 8 = 4k evals per "pass" with accept: nice strong descent: cheap and effective. Hmm, per-eval ~10µs → pass ~40ms → 25 passes/sec.

 I think the final answer is the combination: (A) "fast randomized greedy" (default random pair: plateau-friendly tiebreak on L; acceptance of m-decrement), (B) "targeted kill" proposal (30-50% of trials), (C) periodic kicks (random r-pair move on 60s no-improve + 5% degrade-accept 4-8 steps then re-climb), (D) multi-start diversity: parent-derived, parent-complement, Thue-Morse + complement, random-20% pattern, "block" pattern (worst stride), for a few n (512, 1024, 2048?) — 2048 is 2x cost per trial, only if time.

Continuous phase: L-BFGS-B on g(h) smooth: takes ~1.5-3s per n=1024, 8-15 runs from top seeds: ~30-45s? Plus final. Fine.

Final: strict re-evaluate best candidate via evaluate_erdos_solution? "pre-imported" — can call (guarding): trust my own strict evaluate via np.correlate (text is explicit about the same formula). Also strictly check constraint; repair if needed.

import numpy as np, scipy if needed, from scipy.optimize import minimize, time.

Globals: _BEST = None, etc. Top-level functions (no closures/lambdas).

Strict functions:
def _strict_c5(h): n = len(h); dx = 2.0/n; co = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode = 'full'); return float(np.max(co)) * dx

def _make_binary_feasible(h, n): round to 0/1. Round errors: set n/2 largest to 1.

Initializers:
def _thue_morse(n, base = 0): bitcount of (i + offset)? for i…: parity of bitcount.
Complex pattern via rng.
def _up(parent, n) bilinear.
def _ws_init(...) weighted-section car.
def _banded_init(n, rng) and cycling patterns.
Then _to_binary (t 0.5, deterministic tiebreak by rng).

COST class (global variables operating in functions, avoiding class? class allowed? "helper function must be top-level, no closures/lambdas" — class is OK). For speed, a plain function with module-level globals set per n — but multiple n's run serially? Encode n into the function args — per-call args (n, U, Z, C) → reduced state struct dict? Per-trial dict lookup is a bit slower; module globals are fastest (Python loads per loop are fine). Make a global of G_STATE and per-creation helper functions — but only one state is active so safe. Or _Engine class with instance methods (attribute lookup slightly slower but fine: attribute ~50ns × a few per trial). Actually simple and fast: pass h and n and scratch from a module-level singleton E = _Engine(). Create per n, switch on need. Attribute on an instance (D, G, C...) is compact: self.D etc. Python per-attr per trial: ~8 lookups × 60ns = 0.5µs fine.

Engine supports multiple parallel? No, serial.

Continuous refinement (function):
def _polish_continuous(h0, n, budget, seed): starting float x = float (h0): L-BFGS minimize smooth max. Objective:
 def _sm_obj_grad(x, Ex?): simple: x (length n) global variables: active = Cint(Ash top, clamped) set: Nt = 48, inv weight β = 40, per-eval: bit; Dv = fft rope → C float: smooth max at (i.e., z = ellipse − c_max…OK).
 True C float (h val): express: C_total: C_plus (10) and C minus (01): build: h (float): Cplus_s = M_s − D_s, M = P[n −1 − k]; D via FFT; Mminus = (n/2) − P[k − 1] − D...min list construction: for each k arry [0..511] (C_plus, k, C_minus, k): float 1024 length ✓ smooth max: m0 = C.f; z = β * (C.f − m0); z (z > 20 clip 20); s = float(sum(exp(z))); val = m0 + log(s)/β; grad: w = exp(z)/s (softmax); g = w @ J (w length 1024, J (1024, n) rows: each row is sparse ± pattern: J @ w, row-wise computation: k rows: J row: M_s: +1 for x < n−1−s?
```python
import math
import time
import random
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize

EPS = 1.0e-9
RNG = np.random.RandomState(1)
C2_RNG = np.random.RandomState(777)
G_CAND = []
_U_PL = None
_POL = None


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# exact objective (identical formula to the evaluator)
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
def _eval_c5(h, n=None):
    if n is None:
        n = len(h)
    co = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')
    return float(np.max(co)) * (2.0 / float(n))


def _h_from_u(u, n):
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    h[u] = 1.0
    return h


def _to_u(h, n):
    b = (h >= 0.5)
    s = int(b.sum())
    t = n // 2
    if s != t:
        d = np.abs(h - 0.5)
        idx = np.argsort(d, kind='stable')
        if s > t:
            for k in range(s - t):
                b[int(idx[k])] = False
        else:
            for k in range(s, t):
                b[int(idx[k])] = True
    return np.nonzero(b)[0].astype(np.int32)


def _up_recon(u, n):
    a = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.int32)
    b = n - 1
    for w in range(1, n):
        m1 = u[u >= w]
        if m1.size:
            np.add.at(a, b + m1, 1)
        m2 = u[u <= n - 1 - w]
        if m2.size:
            np.add.at(a, b - w + m2, 1)
    return a


def _hh(n, seed2):
    r = np.random.RandomState(seed2)
    return (r.rand(n) < 0.5).astype(np.float64)


def _tm(n, r):
    m = 1
    while m < n:
        m <<= 1
    off = int(r.randint(m))
    x = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64) + off
    x %= m
    b = ((x & 1) ^ ((x >> 1) & 1) ^ ((x >> 2) & 1) ^ ((x >> 3) & 1) ^
         ((x >> 4) & 1) ^ ((x >> 5) & 1) ^ ((x >> 6) & 1) ^ ((x >> 7) & 1) ^
         ((x >> 8) & 1) ^ ((x >> 9) & 1) ^ ((x >> 10) & 1) ^ ((x >> 11) & 1) ^
         ((x >> 12) & 1) ^ ((x >> 13) & 1) ^ ((x >> 14) & 1) ^ ((x >> 15) & 1))
    b = (b & np.ones(n, dtype=np.int64)).astype(np.float64)
    return _to_u(b, n)


def _wall(n, r):
    segs = [
        (1, int(r.randint(max(5, n // 72), max(10, n // 30)))),
        (0, int(r.randint(max(4, n // 150), max(8, n // 50)))),
        (0, int(r.randint(max(4, n // 130), max(8, n // 45)))),
        (1, int(r.randint(max(5, n // 60), max(10, n // 28)))),
        (0, int(r.randint(max(4, n // 110), max(8, n // 40)))),
        (1, int(r.randint(max(5, n // 90), max(10, n // 36)))),
    ]
    perm = list(range(len(segs)))
    r.shuffle(perm)
    h = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    i = 0
    for q in perm:
        v, ln = segs[q]
        if i >= n:
            break
        h[i:min(n, i + ln)] = float(v)
        i += ln
    return _to_u(h, n)


def _load_parent():
    try:
        a = np.asarray(initial_h_values, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if a.size >= 8 and np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
            return a.copy()
    except Exception:
        pass
    return None


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# engine: integer exact bookkeeping, O(n) per swap trial
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
class _Eng(object):
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = n
        self.b = n - 1
        self.u = None
        self.z = None
        self.p = None
        self.b32 = None
        self.a = None
        self.bs = None
        self.d = None
        self.m = 0
        self.l = 0
        self.ml = 0
        self.ll = 0
        self.bad = 0
        self.best_state = None

    def reset(self, h):
        n = self.n
        u = _to_u(h, n)
        ar = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32)
        self.u = u
        self.z = np.setdiff1d(ar, u, assume_unique=True)
        self.b32 = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.int32)
        self.b32[u] = 1
        self.p = np.cumsum(self.b32)
        self.a = _up_recon(u, n)
        self.bs = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.int32)
        self.d = np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.int32)
        self.m = int(self.a.max())
        self.l = int((self.a == self.m).sum())
        self.ml = self.m
        self.ll = self.l
        self.bad = 0
        self.best_state = self._snap()

    def _snap(self):
        return (self.m, self.l, self.u.copy(), self.z.copy(),
                self.p.copy(), self.a.copy(), self.bs.copy(), self.b32.copy())

    def _load(self, st):
        self.m, self.l = st[0], st[1]
        self.u, self.z, self.p = st[2].copy(), st[3].copy(), st[4].copy()
        self.a, self.bs, self.b32 = st[5].copy(), st[6].copy(), st[7].copy()

    def _try(self, i, j):
        n = self.n
        bas = self.b
        u32 = self.u
        z32 = self.z
        p32 = self.p
        cu = int(p32[i]) - 1
        cz = int(i - cu)
        iuj = int(p32[j])
        izj = int(j - iuj)
        d = self.d
        d.fill(0)
        np.add.at(d, np.concatenate((bas + (z32[izj:] - j), i - iuj + z32[:izj],
                                     u32[iuj:] - bas + j, u32[:iu_j := iuj] + (bas - j))), 1)
        np.add.at(d, np.concatenate((i - cu + z32[:cz], i - cz + z32[cz:],
                                     bas + i + 1 - u32[cu + 1:], bas - i - 1 + u32[:cu])), -1)
        if i < j:
            w = j - i
            d[bas + w] += 1
        else:
            w = i - j
            d[bas - w] += 1
            d[bas + w] += 1
        np.add(self.a, d, out=self.bs)
        m2 = int(self.bs.max())
        if m2 < self.m:
            self._apply(i, j, m2, int((self.bs == m2).sum()))
            return True
        if m2 == self.m:
            l2 = int((self.bs == m2).sum())
            if l2 < self.l:
                self._apply(i, j, m2, l2)
                return True
        return False

    def _apply(self, i, j, m2, l2):
        cu = int(self.p[i]) - 1
        izj = int(j - int(self.p[j]))
        t1 = np.concatenate((self.u[:cu], self.u[cu + 1:]))
        self.u = np.insert(t1, int(np.searchsorted(t1, j)), j)
        t2 = np.concatenate((self.z[:izj], self.z[izj + 1:]))
        self.z = np.insert(t2, int(np.searchsorted(t2, i)), i)
        self.b32[i] = 0
        self.b32[j] = 1
        self.p = np.cumsum(self.b32)
        self.a, self.bs = self.bs, self.a
        self.m, self.l = m2, l2
        self.bad = 0
        if m2 < self.ml or (m2 == self.ml and l2 < self.ll):
            self.ml, self.ll = m2, l2
            self.best_state = self._snap()

    def _comp(self):
        z = self.z
        ac = _up_recon(z, self.n)
        m2 = int(ac.max())
        l2 = int((ac == m2).sum())
        if m2 < self.ml or (m2 == self.ml and l2 < self.ll):
            u, z = z.copy(), self.u.copy()
            b32 = np.zeros(self.n, dtype=np.int32)
            b32[u] = 1
            self.u, self.z, self.b32 = u, z, b32
            self.p = np.cumsum(b32)
            self.a, self.bs = ac.copy(), self.a.copy()
            self.m, self.l, self.ml, self.ll = m2, l2, m2, l2
            self.bad = 0
            self.best_state = self._snap()
            return True
        return False

    def verify(self):
        h = np.zeros(self.n, dtype=np.int32)
        h[self.u] = 1
        cc = np.correlate(h, 1 - h, mode='full')
        return bool(np.array_equal(cc[::-1], self.a))

    def _props(self, abrv, clochant):
        n = self.n
        a = self.a
        m = self.m
        u = self.u
        z = self.z
        b32 = self.b32
        r = RNG
        if abrv:
            pv = np.flatnonzero(a == m)
            if pv.size == 0:
                return None
            mode = r.randint(100)
            if mode < 45:
                return int(u[r.randint(u.size)]), int(z[r.randint(z.size)])
            if mode < 65:
                s = int(pv[r.randint(pv.size)])
                w = s - (n - 1)
                if w <= 0 or w >= n:
                    return int(u[r.randint(u.size)]), int(z[r.randint(z.size)])
                for _ in range(8):
                    p = int(r.randint(n - w))
                    if b32[p] == 0 and b32[p + w] == 1:
                        return int(p + w), p
                return int(u[r.randint(u.size)]), int(z[r.randint(z.size)])
            if mode < 85:
                s = int(pv[r.randint(pv.size)])
                w = s - (n - 1)
                if w >= 0 or w <= -n:
                    return int(u[r.randint(u.size)]), int(z[r.randint(z.size)])
                for _ in range(8):
                    q = int(r.randint(u.size))
                    p = int(u[q])
                    if p - w < n and b32[p - w] == 0:
                        return p, int(p - w)
                for _ in range(4):
                    q = int(r.randint(u.size))
                    p = int(u[q])
                    if p - w < n and b32[p - w] == 0:
                        return p, int(z[r.randint(z.size)])
                return int(u[r.randint(u.size)]), int(z[r.randint(z.size)])
            s = int(pv[r.randint(pv.size)])
            w = s - (n - 1)
            if 0 < w < n:
                for _ in range(6):
                    p = int(r.randint(n - w))
                    if b32[p] == 0 and b32[p + w] == 1:
                        return int(p + w), p
            if -n < w < 0:
                w2 = -w
                for _ in range(6):
                    q = int(r.randint(u.size))
                    p = int(u[q])
                    if p + w2 < n and b32[p + w2] == 0:
                        return p, int(p + w2)
            return int(u[r.randint(u.size)]), int(z[r.randint(z.size)])
        else:
            pv = np.flatnonzero(a == m)
            if pv.size:
                s = int(pv[r.randint(pv.size)])
                w = s - (n - 1)
                if 0 < w < n:
                    for _ in range(6):
                        p = int(r.randint(n - w))
                        if b32[p] == 0 and b32[p + w] == 1:
                            return int(p + w), p
                if -n < w < 0:
                    w2 = -w
                    for _ in range(6):
                        q = int(r.randint(u.size))
                        p = int(u[q])
                        if p + w2 < n and b32[p + w2] == 0:
                            return p, int(p + w2)
            return int(u[r.randint(u.size)]), int(z[r.randint(z.size)])

    def search(self, ops, tend, tight):
        u = self.u
        z = self.z
        kth = int(max(3000, (self.n // 256) * 9000))
        kt = 1
        last = time.time()
        for op in range(ops):
            if (op & 1023) == 0:
                if time.time() >= tend:
                    break
                if (op % 2048) == 0:
                    self._comp()
                    u, z = self.u, self.z
                    if self.ml < self.m or (self.ml == self.m):
                        pass
            if not tight and self.bad > kth and (op & 511) == 0:
                st = self.best_state
                self._load(st)
                u, z = self.u, self.z
                k = 6 + RNG.randint(0, 5 + kt)
                for _ in range(k):
                    self._try(int(u[RNG.randint(u.size)]), int(z[RNG.randint(z.size)]))
                u, z = self.u, self.z
                if time.time() - last > 0.5:
                    kt = kt + 1
                kth = int(kth * 1.25)
                self.bad = 0
                continue
            pr = self._props(tight, kt)
            if pr is None:
                break
            self._try(pr[0], pr[1])
            if (op & 16383) == 0:
                if not self.verify():
                    self.reset(_h_from_u(self.u, self.n))
                    u, z = self.u, self.z
                    self.ml, self.ll = self.m, self.l
                    self.best_state = self._snap()


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# continuous polishing (smoothed max + sum penalty), all helpers top level
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
def _poly_setup(n, beta):
    global _POL
    jw = np.zeros((2 * n, n), dtype=np.float32)
    jw[0, :] = 1.0
    for w in range(1, n):
        jw[2 * w, :n - w] = 1.0
        jw[2 * w + 1, w:] = 1.0
    _POL = (n, float(beta), jw)


def _poly_fg(x):
    n, beta, jw = _POL
    x64 = np.clip(np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64), 0.0, 1.0)
    h = x64.astype(np.float32)
    aa = np.fft.rfft(h, 2 * n)
    dd = np.fft.irfft(aa * aa.conj(), 2 * n)[:n]
    pf = np.cumsum(x64)
    vf = np.empty(2 * n, dtype=np.float64)
    vf[0] = float(x64.sum()) - float((x64 * x64).sum())
    for w in range(1, n):
        vw = dd[w]
        vf[2 * w] = 0.5 * n - pf[w - 1] - vw
        vf[2 * w + 1] = pf[n - 1 - int((vf - vw) * 0 - 0) + int(-w) if False else 0]
    # (recompute cleanly to avoid index confusion)
    for w in range(1, n):
        vw = dd[w]
        vf[2 * w] = 0.5 * n - float(pf[w - 1]) - vw
        vf[2 * w + 1] = float(pf[n - 1 - w]) - vw
    mm = float(vf.max())
    zz = beta * (vf - mm)
    np.minimum(zz, 25.0, out=zz)
    ee = np.exp(zz)
    ss = float(ee.sum())
    fv = mm + math.log(ss) / beta
    wg = ee / ss
    fw = jw.copy()
    on = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float32)
    np.subtract(on, 2.0 * h, out=fw[0])
    for w in range(1, n):
        dg = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float32)
        np.add(dg[:n - w], h[w:], out=dg[:n - w])
        np.add(dg[w:], h[:n - w], out=dg[w:])
        np.subtract(jw[2 * w], dg, out=fw[2 * w])
        np.subtract(jw[2 * w + 1], dg, out=fw[2 * w + 1])
    gr = fw.T @ wg
    sm = float(x64.sum() - 0.5 * n)
    fv += 3000.0 * sm * sm
    gr = gr + 6000.0 * sm
    return (fv, gr)


def _polish(h, n, tb):
    h0 = np.clip(np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64), 0.0, 1.0).copy()
    s = h0.sum()
    if s != 0.5 * n:
        slack = h0[(h0 > 1e-6) & (h0 < 1.0 - 1e-6)]
        if slack.size:
            h0[(h0 > 1e-6) & (h0 < 1.0 - 1e-6)] += (0.5 * n - s) / slack.size
            h0 = np.clip(h0, 0.0, 1.0)
    best = h0.copy()
    bv = _eval_c5(best, n)
    t0 = time.time()
    for beta, it in ((24, 260), (64, 220), (150, 170)):
        if time.time() >= tb or time.time() - t0 > tb - 1:
            break
        if tb <= time.time():
            break
        _poly_setup(n, beta)
        try:
            res = minimize(_poly_fg, best, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B',
                           options={'maxiter': it, 'ftol': 1e-13})
        except Exception:
            continue
        x = np.clip(res.x, 0.0, 1.0)
        dr = 0.5 * n - float(x.sum())
        if abs(dr) > 1e-9:
            slack = x[(x > 1e-5) & (x < 1.0 - 1e-5)]
            if slack.size:
                x[(x > 1e-5) & (x < 1.0 - 1e-5)] += dr / slack.size
                x = np.clip(x, 0.0, 1.0)
        xv = _eval_c5(x, n)
        if xv < bv:
            best = x
            bv = xv
    return (best, bv)


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# verification self test of the incremental engine
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
def _selftest():
    n = 96
    r = np.random.RandomState(55)
    h = (r.rand(n) < 0.5).astype(np.float64)
    u = _to_u(h, n)
    e = _Eng(n)
    e.reset(h)
    cur = e.b32.astype(np.int32).copy()
    for _ in range(400):
        ui = int(r.randint(e.u.size))
        zi = int(r.randint(e.z.size))
        i = int(e.u[ui])
        j = int(e.z[zi])
        e._try(i, j)
        cur[i] = 0
        cur[j] = 1
        cc = np.correlate(cur, 1 - cur, mode='full')
        if not np.array_equal(cc[::-1], e.a):
            return False
    return True


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# stage drivers
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
def _push_cand(h):
    try:
        G_CAND.append((_eval_c5(h), h))
    except Exception:
        pass


def _stage(n, parent, prev_u, t0, budget, idx):
    starts = []
    if prev_u is not None:
        no = len(prev_u)
        if n % no == 0 and n // no >= 2:
            hm = _h_from_u(prev_u, no)
            starts.append(np.repeat(hm, n // no).copy())
    if parent is not None:
        np_ = len(parent)
        if n % np_ == 0 and n // np_ >= 1:
            starts.append(np.repeat(parent, n // np_).copy())
    starts.append(_wall(n, RNG))
    starts.append(1.0 - _wall(n, RNG))
    starts.append(_tm(n, RNG))
    starts.append(_hh(n, 900 + idx * 7))
    starts.append(_hh(n, 41000 + idx * 13))
    starts.append(_hh(n, 80123 + idx * 31))

    nvals = {256: 140000, 512: 60000, 1024: 26000, 2048: 10000}
    ops = nvals.get(n, 20000)
    e = _Eng(n)
    best = None
    n_seeds = len(starts)
    for si, hs in enumerate(starts):
        tl = budget - 30 - (time.time() - t0)
        if tl < 12:
            break
        e.reset(hs)
        e.ml, e.ll = e.m, e.l
        e.best_state = e._snap()
        per = t0 + tl / float(n_seeds - si)
        tight = (si >= 5)
        e.search(ops, min(per, t0 + tl), tight)
        hb = _h_from_u(e.u, n)
        if best is None or _eval_c5(hb, n) < best[0]:
            best = (_eval_c5(hb, n), hb.copy())
    return best


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    global RNG
    RNG = np.random.RandomState(int(seed))
    t_start = time.time()
    try:
        budget_f = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget_f = 1300.0
    if budget_f <= 60.0:
        budget_f = max(30.0, budget_f)
    try:
        if not _selftest():
            RNG
    except Exception:
        pass

    parent = _load_parent()
    if parent is None:
        n_p = 256
        parent = _hh(n_p, 4211)
    else:
        n_p = int(len(parent))

    push = []
    push.append(_eval_c5(parent, n_p))
    _push_cand(parent)

    stages = []
    n_s = n_p
    while n_s <= 2048:
        stages.append(n_s)
        n_s *= 2

    plan = {256: 14.0, 512: 46.0, 1024: 430.0, 2048: 170.0}
    idx = 0
    prev_u = None
    for ns in stages:
        tl = budget_f - 30 - (time.time() - t_start)
        later = 0.0
        for nm in stages[stages.index(ns) + 1:]:
            later += plan.get(nm, 120.0) * 0.6
        tl2 = budget_f - 30 - (time.time() - t_start) - later - 150.0
        tm = min(plan.get(ns, 60.0), max(8.0, tl2))
        if tl < 20.0:
            break
        if tm < 8.0:
            continue
        res = _stage(ns, parent, prev_u, t_start, t_start + tm, idx)
        idx += 1
        if res is not None and res[0] < min(push) + 1.0:
            _push_cand(res[1])
            push.append(res[0])
            prev_u = _to_u(res[1], ns)

    tl = budget_f - 40 - (time.time() - t_start)
    tc = min(max(30.0, tl), 300.0)
    if tl > 60.0:
        pool = list(G_CAND[:])
        pool.sort(key=lambda z: z[0])
        seen = set()
        chosen = []
        for cv, ch in pool:
            key = int(round(cv * 100000))
            if key in seen:
                continue
            seen.add(key)
            chosen.append(ch)
            if len(chosen) >= 4:
                break
        n_pick = len(chosen)
        each = tc / float(max(1, n_pick))
        for k, ch in enumerate(chosen):
            tleft = budget_f - 30 - (time.time() - t_start)
            if tleft < 25.0:
                break
            try:
                hh, vv = _polish(ch, len(ch), t_start + min(each, tleft))
                if vv < min(push) if push else True:
                    _push_cand(hh.copy())
                    push.append(vv)
            except Exception:
                pass

    pool = list(G_CAND[:])
    if pool:
        pool.sort(key=lambda z: z[0])
        for cv, ch in pool:
            tleft = budget_f - 25 - (time.time() - t_start)
            if tleft < 6.0:
                break
            hv = np.clip(np.asarray(ch, dtype=np.float64), 0.0, 1.0)
            nv = len(hv)
            ds = float(hv.sum() - 0.5 * nv)
            if abs(ds) > 1e-9:
                slack = hv[(hv > 1e-6) & (hv < 1.0 - 1e-6)]
                if slack.size:
                    hv[(hv > 1e-6) & (hv < 1.0 - 1e-6)] -= ds / slack.size
            try:
                ev = _eval_c5(hv, nv)
            except Exception:
                break
            pool[pool.index((cv, ch))] = (ev, hv)
        pool.sort(key=lambda z: z[0])
        return (pool[0][1], float(pool[0][0]), int(len(pool[0][1])))
    fv = _eval_c5(_hh(256, 1), 256)
    return (_hh(256, 1), fv, 256)
```
